22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})\leq\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x,\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*}),

(4.14)

and the results in (
4.12
)-(

) indicates the maxima inequality

\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})

\leq

\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})

with respect to
({\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})
. Due to the decoupling of
\lambda
with
({\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})
in the PAL (
4.4
), this inequality holds for all
\lambda
rather than only on
\lambda^{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})\leq\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*}).

(4.15)

Inserting
(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
into (
4.10b
) give
\lambda^{*}\odot g(x^{*})=0
; i.e.,
\lambda_{i}^{*}g_{i}(x^{*})=0,i=1,\cdots,r
. This shows that
g_{i}(x^{*})=0
or
\lambda_{i}^{*}=0
. The former is nothing but
\partial\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})/\partial\lambda=0
so that the function
\lambda\rightarrow\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
is maximized at
\lambda^{*}
; the latter, together with
g_{i}(x^{*})\leq 0
, shows that the function
\lambda\rightarrow\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
is maximized at
\lambda^{*}
. Both cases indicate that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})\leq\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*}).

(4.16)

The combination of (
4.15
) and (

) gives

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern (4.17)

The two inequalities (
4.14
) and (
4.17
) tell us that
(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
is the saddle point of
\mathscr{L}_{\mu}
. According to the first result in Lemma
4.2
,
x^{*}
is an optimal solution to the optimization problem (
3.2
).
\blacksquare

With the relationship between the equilibrium of the optimization dynamics (
4.10
) and the optimal solution to the problem (
3.2
) discovered in the above lemma, we have the following result which proves the exponential stability of our algorithm (
4.10
).

Theorem 4.5

For the optimization problem (
3.2
) under Assumptions
1
-
3
, consider the optimization algorithm (
4.10
). Then, for any trajectory of (
4.10
) with initial conditions
x(0)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
(\lambda(0),{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}(0),{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}(0))\in\mathbb{R}^{r}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
, the sub-trajectory
x(t)
converges to the optimal solution
x^{*}
exponentially as
t\rightarrow+\infty
.

Proof:
Construct a Lyapunov candidate
V(x;\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})=V_{1}+V_{2}+V_{3}+V_{4}
with

\displaystyle V_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\|x-x^{*}\|^{2},

\displaystyle V_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{r}\eta_{i}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*})^{2},

\displaystyle V_{3}=\sum\nolimits_{i\in\Omega}D_{\psi}(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i}^{*})+\sum\nolimits_{i\notin\Omega}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*})^{2},

\displaystyle V_{4}=\frac{1}{2}\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{s}({\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}-{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}^{*})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{s}({\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}-{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar where
\Omega=\{i|\lambda_{i}^{*}\!\neq\!0\}
and
D_{\psi}(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i}^{*})\geq 0
is the Bregman divergence
bregman1967
between
\lambda_{i}
and
\lambda_{i}^{*}
with respect to

.
Therefore,
V\geq 0
, and also
V(x;\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})=0
if and only if
(x;\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
. We now use this candidate Lyapunov function to prove exponential stability of the equilibrium
(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
of the system (
4.10
) by referring to the criterion in
(
khalil1996
, Corollary 3.4)
. To this end, we will show that
V
is lower and upper bounded by quadratic functions and the time derivative of
V
is upper bounded by a negative quadratic function. The following two parts are the details.

(1) We first show that
V
can be upper and lower bounded by quadratic functions of
(x;\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})
(we only prove this for
V_{3}
since this is obvious for
V_{1},V_{2},V_{4}
).
For
i\in\Omega
which implies
\lambda_{i}^{*}>0
, the Bregman
D_{\psi}(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i}^{*})
can be calculated as

\displaystyle D_{\psi}(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i}^{*})=\psi(\lambda_{i})-\psi(\lambda_{i}^{*})-\psi^{\prime}(\lambda_{i}^{*})(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*})=\frac{1}{2\varsigma_{i}}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*})^{2}.

with
\varsigma_{i}
lying between
\lambda_{i}^{*}
and
\lambda_{i}
. In view of
\lambda_{i}^{*}>0
and
\lambda_{i}\rightarrow\lambda_{i}^{*}
,
\varsigma_{i}
is a positive constant. Denoting
\tilde{a}=\underset{i\in\Omega}{{\rm min}}\{1/2\varsigma_{i}\}
and
\tilde{b}=\underset{i\in\Omega}{\max}\{1/2\varsigma_{i}\}
, one has
\tilde{a}\leq D_{\psi}(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i}^{*})\leq\tilde{b}
. With this, one can obtain the following lower and upper quadratic bounds for
V_{3}
,

\displaystyle a\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j\in\Omega}\tilde{a}|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*}|^{2}+\sum\limits_{j\notin\Omega}(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j}^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq V_{3}\leq\sum\limits_{j\in\Omega}\tilde{b}|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{*}|^{2}+\sum\limits_{j\notin\Omega}(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j}^{*})^{2}\leq b\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}\|^{2},

where
a={\rm min}(\tilde{a},1)>0
and
b=\max(\tilde{b},1)>0
. Therefore, the function
V
has
quadratic lower and upper bounds.

and strongly concave in
(\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})
, the above inequality becomes

\displaystyle\dot{V}|_{(\ref{proxPD})}

\displaystyle\leq[\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x^{*};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})-\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x;\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})]+[\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x;\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})-\mathscr{L}_{\mu}(x;\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\alpha\|x-x^{*}\|^{2}-\left(\frac{\mu\ell}{\mu+\ell}+2\mu\right)\|\Lambda-\Lambda^{*}\|^{2}.

(4.18)

This shows that
\dot{V}
has minus quadratic upper bound.

\blacksquare

Usually, in designing algorithms for constrained optimization
problem, either hard constraints or soft constraints can be considered. In the former, the
optimization constraints should be satisfied on the fly (e.g., the interior algorithms),
while in the latter, rather than forcing each transient state of the iterate to obey the constraints, one requires the optimization constraints to be satisfied only at the asymptotic
state. Since the ultimate objective is to compute the optimal solution that satisfies the constraints, our algorithm just follows the second line of soft constraints and therefore there is
no need in this paper to show positive feasibility for each transient state
\lambda(t)
but feasibility for the asymptotic state of
\lambda(t)
only. This is indeed the case in view of the equilibrium analysis in Lemma
4.4
.

Remark 4.6

We note that our method is capable of achieving fully smooth dynamics while handling general convex inequality constraints.
In primal-dual optimization dynamics based on the PAL framework, the multiplier
\lambda
associated with inequality constraint must remain nonnegative. Existing methods enforce this by incorporating a projection onto the positive quadrant
feijer2010
;
goldsztajn2021
;
tang2020
, but this introduces nonsmooth dynamics that complicate stability analysis.
To address this, smooth alternatives have been proposed to eliminate projection.
The works
qu2019
;
adegbege2021
;
bin2024
handle inequality constraints without explicit projection, instead they replace projection-based updates with max-based regularization (specifically algorithm (9a)-(9c) in
adegbege2021
, (9a)-(9b) in
qu2019
, and (1b) in
bin2024
), implicitly ensuring nonnegativity of the multiplier associated with inequality constraint. While this improves computational simplicity, the max operator still makes the optimization dynamics nonsmooth in the classical sense.
In contrast, our projection-free dynamics (
4.10b
) is fully smooth, and this smooth nature enables rigorous stability certification via classical Lyapunov techniques, bypassing the need for nonsmooth analysis tools like differential inclusions.
More important, the max-based method in
qu2019
;
adegbege2021
are limited to affine inequality constraints, with no extension to general convex inequality constraints. Our approach overcomes this limitation and extends to general convex inequality constraints.

Remark 4.7

Asides from its ability to obtain fully smooth optimization dynamics and deal with general convex inequality constraints, our method can yield exponential convergence rather than asymptotic convergence.
As noted in
wang2021
, the combination of general convex constraints and nonsmooth objectives poses a significant challenge to achieving exponential stability. For instance, existing studies on general inequality constraints typically ensure only asymptotic convergence
goldsztajn2021
or semi-global exponential convergence
tang2020
. IQC is a widely used framework for exponential convergence analysis.
However, existing IQC-based approaches primarily address affine-constrained settings, such as equality constraints
dhingra2019
;
hassan2020
;
hassan2021
;
wang2021
;
ding2020
or affine linear inequalities
hassan2018
;
bansode2019
, leaving nonlinear convex inequality constraints unexamined. Similar limitations appear in projection-free methods
adegbege2021
and exponential stability analyses
qu2019
, which remain restricted to affine inequalities.
We do not use the IQC method, we can prove exponential stability without assuming affine linearity assumption on inequality constraints.

Note that, in the proof of Theorem
4.5
, the strong concavity of the PAL function
\mathscr{L}_{\mu}
on
(\lambda,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}})
depends only on the Lipschitz continuous of

in the sense of Assumption

) is dropped due to the lack of strong convexity of

. Since
(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
is a saddle point of

, the two curl brackets are both nonnegative. Therefore, the right hand side of (

) is less than or equal to zero. Thus

.

to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
. As a consequence of LaSalle’s invariance principle, the equilibrium
(x^{*};\lambda^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}^{*})
is asymptotically stable.
\blacksquare

Remark 4.9

Although small values of the regularization parameter
\mu
would cause slow convergence, this slowness can be alleviated via the “continuation schemes” carried out in the following way. Firstly, given a particular parameter
\mu
, one runs the algorithm to obtain a solution. With this solution as the “warm-start” and taking it as the initial state of the second round of execution of the optimization dynamics with a smaller parameter
\mu
, one obtains a second solution. Continuing this procedure with a sequence of decreasing
\mu
, the optimal solution can be computed in succession. This kind of “continuation schemes” have been found quite effective in speeding up the algorithm
figueiredo2007
;
afonso2010
.

5
Simulation example

As an example, we applied our algorithm to the following distributed optimization problem on a network with
N
nodes,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}{\rm minimize}&&\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(x),\\
{\rm subject~to}&&g_{i}(x)\preccurlyeq 0,\\
&&h_{i}(x)=0,i=1,\cdots,N.\end{array}\right.

Here
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the decision variable,
f_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the local cost function on node
i
,

g_{i}=(g_{i1},\cdots,g_{ir_{i}})^{\top}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{r_{i}}
is the inequality constrained function on node
i
, and

h_{i}=(h_{i1},\cdots,h_{is_{i}})^{\top}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{s_{i}}
is the equality constrained function on node
i
. If there is no constraints for agent
i
, one simply sets corresponding constraint functions to be zero.

In view of large
N
, it is difficult for a single agent to do the optimization task. Instead, one usually uses
N
agents to cooperatively solve the problem, where each agent
i
executes a subtask of local optimization by only using information from agent
i
(i.e., the information
f_{i},g_{i},h_{i}
as well as their gradients) and those information from its neighbors
\mathcal{N}_{i}
. The neighboring relationship encodes the cooperation among these agents and is described by a graph. The cooperation makes local optimization algorithms compute the optimal solution in a consensus way. That is, letting
x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
denote the estimation of the optimal solution
x^{*}
by local algorithm of agent
i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
, the cooperation among these agents should be directed toward rendering
x_{1}=x_{2}=\cdots=x_{N}=x^{*}
.
Denoting respectively the graph Laplacian and incidence matrix of the graph by
L
and
T
so that
L=T^{\top}T
, the consensus
x_{1}=x_{2}=\cdots=x_{N}
among agents can be enforce by imposing the equality constraint
(T\otimes I_{n})\mbox{\boldmath$x$}=0
with
\mbox{\boldmath$x$}={\rm col}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{nN}
if the graph is connected.
By adding to the objective function in (
5
) the following penalty function

\displaystyle\varphi(T\mbox{\boldmath$x$})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lllll}&0,&T\mbox{\boldmath$x$}=0\\
&+\infty,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

the optimization problem (
5
) can be represented as the following form

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}{\rm minimize}&&\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(x_{i})+\varphi(T\mbox{\boldmath$x$}),\\
{\rm subject~to}&&g_{i}(x_{i})\preccurlyeq 0,\\
&&h_{i}(x_{i})=0,i=1,\cdots,N.\end{array}\right.

Defining the objective function
F(\mbox{\boldmath$x$})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}(x_{i})
and stack constraint functions
G(\mbox{\boldmath$x$})={\rm col}[g_{1}(x_{1}),\cdots,g_{N}(x_{N})]
,
H(\mbox{\boldmath$x$})={\rm col}[h_{1}(x_{1}),\cdots,h_{N}(x_{N})]
, the problem (
5
) can be written in a compact form as

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}{\rm minimize}&&F(\mbox{\boldmath$x$})+\varphi(T\mbox{\boldmath$x$}),\\
{\rm subject~to}&&G(\mbox{\boldmath$x$})\preccurlyeq 0,\\
&&H(\mbox{\boldmath$x$})=0.\end{array}\right.

We now apply the algorithm (
4.10
) to the nonsmooth composite optimization problem (
5
). Note that, for the function
\varphi
defined in (
5
), it is easy to show that
\nabla\varphi(y)=\frac{1}{\mu}y
.
Furthermore, corresponding to the multipliers
\lambda
and
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}
in (
4.10
) for a single agent, we use
\lambda_{i}=(\lambda_{i1},\cdots,\lambda_{ir_{i}})^{\top}
and
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}=({\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i1},\cdots,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{is_{i}})^{\top}
to respectively denote multipliers corresponding to constraints
g_{i}(x)\preceq 0
and
h_{i}(x)=0
; more specifically
\lambda_{ij}
is the multiplier for
g_{ij}(x)\leq 0
for
j=1,\cdots,r_{i}
and
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{ij}
is the multiplier for
h_{ij}(x)=0
for
j=1,\cdots,s_{i}
. Note that if there is no inequality or equality constraint for agent
i
, we simply set
\lambda_{i}
or
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}
to be zero vector of appropriate dimension.
As for the multiplier
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}
(bold lambda used here to account for multiple agents) for the constraints
T\mbox{\boldmath$x$}=\mbox{\boldmath$y$}
in (
4.10
), we introduce a new multiplier
\mbox{\boldmath${\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}$}^{\prime}=T\mbox{\boldmath${\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}$}
with partition
\mbox{\boldmath${\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}$}^{\prime}={\rm col}[{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{N}^{\prime}]
. With this new multiplier, the subequatons (
4.10a
) and (
4.10d
) have a simpler forms. More specifically, the resulting optimization algorithm has the following distributed form

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{i}\!=\!\frac{1}{\mu}\!\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}\!(x_{j}\!-\!x_{i})\!-\!\nabla f_{i}(x_{i})\!-\!\sum_{j}^{r_{i}}\!\lambda_{ij}\nabla g_{ij}(x_{i})\!-\!\sum_{j}^{s_{i}}\!{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{ij}\nabla h_{ij}(x_{i})\!-\!{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}

(5.15a)

\displaystyle\dot{\lambda}_{ij}=\frac{\lambda_{ij}}{1+\eta_{ij}\lambda_{ij}}g_{ij}(x_{i}),\hskip 14.22636ptj=1,\cdots,r_{i},

(5.15b)

\displaystyle\dot{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{ij}=h_{ij}(x_{i}),\hskip 56.9055ptj=1,\cdots,s_{i},

(5.15c)

\displaystyle\dot{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}^{\prime}=-\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{i}}(x_{j}-x_{i}),

(5.15d)

where
\mathcal{N}_{i}=\{j|(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}\}
is the neighboring agents of agent
i
. This algorithm is distributed since the optimization dynamics for agent
i
depends on information from its neighboring agents. See also
dhingra2019
for distributed implementation without considering optimization constraints.

Figure 1:
The topology of five agents described by a graph.

We now apply the above distributed optimization algorithm to the following Rosen-Suzuki problem
charalamous1977

\displaystyle{\rm min}

\displaystyle x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}-5x_{1}-5x_{2}-21x_{3}+7x_{4}

\displaystyle s.t.

\displaystyle-8+x_{1}-x_{2}+x_{3}-x_{4}+x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\leq 0,

\displaystyle-10-x_{1}-x_{4}+x_{1}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+2x_{4}^{2}\leq 0,

\displaystyle-5+2x_{1}-x_{2}-x_{4}+2x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}=0.

The optimal solution is shown in
charalamous1977
to be
x^{*}=(0,1,2,-1)
.

Figure 2:
The time evolution of the states for 5 agents. (a) The first components of the five agents all converge to
0
; (b)The second components of the five agents all converge to
1
; (c) The third components of the five agents all converge to
2
; (d)The fourth components of the five agents all converge to
-1
. Therefore, each state of the 5 agents converges to the optimal solution
(0,1,2,-1)
.

We now use our distributed algorithm (
5.15
) to recover this result.
Note the function corresponding to the equality constraint is not affine which is not satisfied by our theorems. However, since our theorems only give sufficient conditions, this mean that our algorithms may work even if these sufficient conditions are not satisfied. Our simulation shows that this is indeed the case for the above example.
To run the simulation, we use five agents whose coupling is described by the graph in Fig.
1
. The state of each agent lies in
\mathbb{R}^{4}
. To run simulation under this setup, define
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})^{\top}
and let
f_{1}(x)=x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}
,
f_{2}(x)=2x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}
,
f_{3}(x)=-5x_{1}-5x_{2}
,
f_{4}(x)=-21x_{3}
,
f_{5}(x)=7x_{4}
,
g_{1}(x)=-8+x_{1}-x_{2}+x_{3}-x_{4}+x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}
,
g_{2}(x)=-10-x_{1}-x_{4}+x_{1}^{2}+2x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+2x_{4}^{2}
,
h_{1}(x)=-5+2x_{1}-x_{2}-x_{4}+2x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}
.
We assume that agent 1 has only access to
f_{1},g_{1},h_{1}
, agent 2 has only access to
f_{2},g_{2}
, and agent
i
has only access to
f_{i}
for
i=3,4,5
.
The parameters
\eta_{ij}
in (
5.15
) is only required to be positive so that we chose them to be
1
for briefly. The initial states of the five agents are

x_{1}(0)=(3,4,-3,4)^{\top}
,
x_{2}(0)=(1,-2,4,2)^{\top}
,
x_{3}(0)=(-3,-4,3,3)^{\top}
,
x_{4}(0)=(3,1,2,-3)^{\top}
,
x_{5}(0)=(4,-2,-4,1)^{\top}
, the initial states for the multipliers corresponding to the inequality constraints of agents 1 and 2 are given as
\lambda_{1}(0)=\lambda_{2}(0)=3
, the initial state for the multiplier corresponding to the equality constraints of agents 1 is given as
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.07639pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{1}(0)=3
, the initial states for the transformed multiplier
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{i}
of the five agents are given as
{\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{1}(0)=\cdots={\mathchoice{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.72218pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\textstyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-1.93747pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 1.2mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}{\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-0.6458pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 1.0mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\smash{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.22916pt}{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.5mu\mathchar 22$}\hss}}\lambda}}_{5}(0)=(1,2,3,4)^{\top}
. The simulation result, shown in Fig.
2
, indicates that the first, second, third, and fourth components of five agents converge to
0,1,2,-1
respectively. To save place, the time evolutions of the multipliers are not plotted here.

Obviously, we can also use, say 7 agents, to simulate the optimal solution. Which number to use is application depend. Due to space limitation, the simulation result is omitted here.

6
Conclusions

This paper introduces a projection-free optimization dynamics for nonsmooth composite optimization problems involving both equality and convex inequality constraints. Unlike conventional projection-based approaches, which introduce nonsmooth dynamics and involve complicate stability analysis, the proposed method avoids using projection and ensures a fully smooth optimization process. This smoothness allows for rigorous stability analysis using classical Lyapunov techniques, eliminating the need for nonsmooth analysis tools such as differential inclusions.

Although several studies have also attempted to design constrained optimization algorithms without explicitly relying on projection methods, they instead substitute the projection operator with the relatively easier-to-compute max operator, still rendering the resulting optimization dynamics nonsmooth in the classical sense.
Our optimization dynamics, which operates without projection, is entirely smooth and facilitates stability analysis through traditional Lyapunov methods, eliminating the necessity for nonsmooth techniques such as differential inclusions. More importantly, unlike the max-based approach which is confined to affine inequality constraints, our method overcomes this limitation and extends to general convex inequality constraints.

Our algorithm also has the ability to achieve exponential convergence and this represents an advancement over existing works that typically ensure only asymptotic or semi-global exponential convergence. On the other hand, our method follows a new line of exponential convergence analysis. Unlike existing methods of proving exponential stability by using the tool of IQC which are valid only when the additional constraints are linear or affine, this paper extends the PAL framework to incorporate general convex inequality constraints and achieves strong convexity-concavity in the primal-dual variables, coming with the capability of dealing with general convex nonlinear constraints.
